# OPEN FINDINGS — the live register

**This is a CARRIER (`CLAUDE.md` rule 16). It is not yours to compact, prune or
summarise. Rows leave it only by being CLOSED with the measurement that closed
them, or RETIRED with the ruling that retired them — never by being dropped.**

An open-findings register existed once and was **abandoned at rev 45 with 21
rows**, and the loss went unnoticed for eleven revisions. The
standing-instructions carrier went the same way at rev 44 and took the
project's original deliverable with it (**F18** below). This file is the
register reinstated at rev 56, seeded only from findings that could be
grounded in the repository on the day it was written.

## How to use it

* **Every row carries a PROVENANCE GRADE.** That is the point of the file.
  * `MEASURED-revN` — a script in this repo printed it in revision N.
  * `RECOMPUTED-revN` — re-derived from source in revision N, agreeing with an
    earlier figure.
  * `INHERITED-revN` — carried from revision N's prose and **not re-measured
    since**. Treat every one of these as a claim, not a number.
  * `RULED-revN` — the owner decided it.
  * `CEILED` — established as far as the material allows; *"it cannot be
    recovered from what we hold"* is the result.
* **Add rows; do not renumber them.** IDs are permanent. A closed row keeps its
  ID and its number, with `CLOSED-revN` and the measurement that closed it.
* **Grade decay is a finding in itself.** An `INHERITED` row that survives three
  more revisions without being re-measured should be re-measured or downgraded
  to a claim, not quietly re-quoted.
* `verify_clone.sh` asserts this file exists, that the newest brief names it,
  and that every OPEN row carries a grade.

## MERGED FROM `origin/claude/bus-model-rev57-yvrlhi` AT REV 59 — F88–F97

**THE OWNER RULED AT REV 59: *"Merge it, renumber its IDs."*** That branch was pushed
mid-revision at rev 57b and sat stranded for three revisions, 6 commits and 16 files ahead of
`main`, with `bootstrap.sh` row 9 red for exactly this. It used **F58–F67 for DIFFERENT findings
than `main` does**, and `OPEN_FINDINGS.md`'s own rule is that **IDs are permanent** — so `main`'s
F58–F67 keep their numbers and the branch's ten are renumbered here. **Every renumbered row carries
its old ID on the row itself**, so a reader of `LEDGER_rev57.md` or
`NEXT_CONTEXT_PROMPT_rev58.md` — both of which the branch also edited, and both of which still use
its old numbering — can translate:

| on that branch | here, permanently |
|---|---|
| F58 → **F88** | F59 → **F89** | 
| F60 → **F90** | F61 → **F91** |
| F62 → **F92** | F63 → **F93** |
| F64 → **F94** | F65 → **F95** |
| F66 → **F96** | F67 → **F97** |

**WHAT THE MERGE ACTUALLY BROUGHT IN**, since a merge of a branch 15 commits behind would otherwise
revert two revisions of documents: `probe_rev58_ceiling.py` (**on no other branch**), its seven
painted `probe_scratch/rev58_ceil_*.png` masks, the **MIRROR ARM** of `probe_rev58_gloss.py`
(`T1_GL_MIRROR`, which asks the geometry for the reflected direction instead of guessing a pose —
rule 7), and these ten rows. **`PASTE_INTO_CLAUDE_CODE.txt`, `verify_clone.sh`, `audit_adversary.py`
and `README.md` were resolved to HEAD**, because the branch's copies predate rev 58 and rev 59
entirely. `probe_rev58_gloss.py` kept **BOTH** arms — rev 58's dark-card and rev 57b's mirror.

**AND THREE OF THESE WERE ALREADY HALF-RECOVERED.** Rev 58b lifted the owner's three standing bars
off this branch by hand and carried them under `yvrlhi-F61/F63/F64`. Those labels are now the
permanent **F91 / F93 / F94**, and the standing-bars section below uses them.

Brighten the frame and the paint desaturates out of them, so the window RETREATS INTO THE SHADOWS — and what it retreats to is exactly the part that is still well exposed | **OPEN as a limit on the gate, and it does not touch any number the gate has published.** Measured on the over-exposed ceiling frame `out/c3_hero.png`: the mask fell **33,600 px → 5,711 px (17.0 %)**, a strict subset, and read **0.00 % clipped inside the retreat against 53.32 % over the region it came from**. ## Added at rev 58

| ID | finding | status | the number | grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **F58** | **A VERIFIER ROW PASSED ONLY IN THE TREE THAT WROTE IT.** The `gloss_compare is exposure-free` row called the script with no argument; the script's default was a hard-coded `out/r57_hero.png`, and **`out/` is untracked and starts EMPTY on every clone**. So the row passed for its author and failed for everybody else — and it reported the missing file as **`MOVED: []`**, an ABSENT INPUT dressed as a MOVED STATISTIC, which points the reader at the estimator instead of at the path | **CLOSED-rev58.** Exposure invariance is a property of the ESTIMATOR, so it is now a `--selftest` that runs the real `spread()` on a synthetic patch: no frame, no filename to rot. The script also takes the newest hero present instead of a revision-numbered default, and says `NO RENDER` in those words when there is none | **WATCHED FAILING** via `T1_GC_ABSSPREAD=1`, which drops the `/p50` that makes the statistic scale-free: x0.70 **35.8691**, x1.00 **51.2416**, x1.40 **71.7382**, rc=1. *(Those three were typed as a PREDICTION first and were wrong by two orders of magnitude — rule 5, caught in the same edit.)* This is the rev-57b audit's own lesson recurring one revision later, in a verifier row this time | **MEASURED-rev58** |
| **F59** | **THE GLOSS GATE'S HEADROOM WAS A THIRD ARTWORK.** `spread()`'s red mask (`R > G*1.35`) correctly excludes cream, chrome, the silver script and the teal — the kept set is **95.0 %** body red, so the rectangle's raw 42.9 % red fraction OVERSTATES the problem and is not the finding. But **3.4 % of the kept pixels are the gold flank ink, and they are the BRIGHT ones**: the p99 pixels average RGB **(194.8, 119.0, 78.7)**, G/R **0.61**. HEADROOM is defined on p99 | **CLOSED-rev58** by tightening the mask with `G < 0.55R & B < 0.50R`; `T1_GC_LOOSEMASK=1` restores the old one so the change is testable both ways | Excluding the ink moves **SPREAD** render **+0.6 %**, photo **+0.8 %** — *the headline is ROBUST: the ratio reads 0.3918 loose against 0.3911 tight* — and **HEADROOM** render **−31.6 %**, photo **+0.1 %**. *(RECOMPUTED through the shipped path at the close of rev 58, which CORRECTED −0.2 % and −29.4 % published earlier in this revision from an exploratory script that ordered the mask and the erosion differently. Rule 17's other half, doing its job.)* So the render's specular headroom is **0.090** of the photograph's, **not the 0.132 published at rev 57b**. The correction makes the deficit WORSE, not better | **MEASURED-rev58** |
| **F60** | **ROUGHNESS IS THE LIVE LEVER, IT IS 16× THE CLEARCOAT, AND IT SATURATES.** F53 said the BSDF `Roughness` socket is LINKED and inert; the live path is the **WEATHER group's own `Roughness` input**, which `apply_weather()` loads with the BSDF's 0.420 and which sets the band `rgh ∓ W_ROUGH_SWING (0.09)`. `probe_rev58_gloss.py`'s new `T1_GL_WRGH` writes it, and REFUSES if the node or socket is not where the trace says | **OPEN as a DECISION, not a measurement** — the number is settled; whether to ship it is the owner's, because it trades against colour (W6) | spread ratio **0.3911 → 0.4261** at rgh **0.250** (+8.9 %), headroom **0.0899 → 0.1458** (+62 %), chroma cost G/R **0.4315 → 0.4364** (**+1.1 %**). It **SATURATES**: rgh 0.050 gives **0.4233 / 0.1408** — *worse* on gloss and dearer on chroma (G/R 0.4451). Against F54's clearcoat, which bought **+0.5 %** of spread for **+17.9 %** of G/R: **16× the gloss for a fifth of the chroma cost.** Still **FAILS** the 0.60 bar | **MEASURED-rev58** |
| **F61** | **THE RIG-CEILING ARM MEASURES THE WRONG THING, AND ITS DEFAULT HAS NEVER BEEN RUN.** `T1_GL_SPOT` adds small BRIGHT sources on the theory that the photograph's market-hall lamps are what the studio lacks. **Refuted by its own ablation**: adding light adds FILL, which lifts p5 and COMPRESSES the relative spread | **OPEN — the arm cannot attribute the residual gap**, and F55's question is therefore NOT answered by it | 3 × 250 W moved the gate **BACKWARDS**: ratio **0.4261 → 0.3435**, median L **104.7 → 124.2**, G/R **0.4364 → 0.4860**. At the arm's **own default** 3 × 4000 W the panel washed out to median G/R **0.917** and the gate **REFUSED** it (**0** px passed the red mask) — the default was shipped at rev 57b untested. The refusal is the mask working, and it is recorded as such | **MEASURED-rev58** |

| **F62** | **WHAT THIS FLANK ACTUALLY REFLECTS, MEASURED OFF THE SHIPPED CAMERA AND THE SHIPPED GEOMETRY — and it explains F54, F55 and F61 together.** The hero camera sits at **(8.156, 5.603, 1.307)**, i.e. essentially at flank height (the sample point is z 1.05), so the view of the flank is near-grazing. Mirroring the view ray about the flank normal (0,1,0) gives **(−0.8564, +0.5155, −0.0281)**: the flank's specular image is a patch of **cyclorama floor 19.3 m outboard**, near the horizon, and featureless | **OPEN as the stated CEILING on item A.** It is not a defect to fix: making that patch structured means changing what the cyclorama looks like, and *"keep studio, fix the model"* is the owner's ruling | This is why **T1_GL_SPOT fails (F61)**: its lamps sit at z 5.2–6.4 and y ≈ 7, nowhere near the mirror direction, so they can only contribute diffuse FILL — which is exactly what was measured (spread DOWN, median L up). And a dark card placed in the mirror direction is **19 m out and, at a camera 1.31 m off the ground, IN FRAME** — watched: `out/w25dark_hero.png` has the card across two-thirds of the shot and the gate refused it (0 red px). **The residual gloss gap is the SURROUND's, and the model-side lever is exhausted at 0.4261** | **MEASURED-rev58** |

| **F63** | **THE NOSE VW GLYPH BUILDS AS AN X, NOT A V OVER A W — and the project's OWN rasteriser draws it that way.** `probe_rev46_vw.py`'s built raster is an X in a ring with the W's outer arms as stubby triangles that never reach it. **The owner's FIFTH report of this emblem** (*"the vw emblems still need a fix"*), against a probe that has reported *"5 controls, 0 FAILED"* throughout. `t1_core` already carries the rule, written at rev 46 about his FOURTH report: *"HIS REPEAT IS A MEASUREMENT: when he reports the same thing twice the prior closure was wrong or incomplete"* | **OPEN — GATED NOW, and it is the top job.** New control **C6** compares the number of cream cells the strokes cut the ring into: **photograph 7, built 6.** A stroke that fails to reach the ring merges the two cells either side of it, so the deficit of one *is* the floating pair. Structural (a region count), needs no axis ratio, survives blur; photograph and build go through the SAME function. **WATCHED FAILING**, and its KILL works (collapsing the W's arms moves the count 6 → 4) | off the mesh, as a fraction of the ring OUTER R, band inner **0.7988**: V arm tips **0.8400**, W legs **0.8394** — on the band; **W OUTER ARM tips 0.6638, floating 18.9 mm** on a 140.1 mm radius | **MEASURED-rev58** |
| **F64** | **WHY REV 46's SOLVER COULD NOT SEE IT: every landmark it fits is a VERTICAL POSITION and none is a RADIUS.** L1–L6 are row indices down the emblem, so a stroke may terminate 18.9 mm short of the band with every landmark still landing — where a stroke ends vertically and how far it reaches radially are independent quantities. This is rev 46's own discovery, *"the axis nobody checked"*, recurring on the axis rev 46 did not check | **OPEN — the solver needs the reach axis in its objective before it is re-run.** The vertical fit is not wrong and is not discarded: residual **0.0347** against rev 45's 0.1167 still holds | **THE MECHANISM, traced not guessed:** a terminal cap is cut PERPENDICULAR to its stroke, so its two corners sit at different radii; `vw_bars`' fixed point drives each terminal's **MAX** corner onto the band, leaving the other short by the cap's whole radial span. The W's outer arm meets the ring at **0.12°** while travelling at **55.5°**, so that span is **0.176 R** and the far corner lands at 0.6638. Then `vw_logo_fit` re-normalises by the GLOBAL EXTREME — the very mechanism rev 44b named and fixed one stage higher (*"scales by the SINGLE FURTHEST VERTEX … drags every other end short"*), still live one stage below | **MEASURED-rev58** |
| **F65** | **THREE CANDIDATE FIXES FOR F63 WERE TRIED AND ALL THREE FAILED — recorded so the next attempt does not re-spend itself on them** | **OPEN.** The V's arms and the W's outer arms cross the same region BY CONSTRUCTION, so this is a **re-solve of the W's spine against reach**, not a one-constant tweak. Nothing was shipped: the tree is back at HEAD for both files | drive the **MIN** cap corner instead of the MAX → cells 6 → **4** (worse); make `vw_logo_fit` a **pure unit conversion** → 6 → **4** (worse); raise **`VW_W_ARM_Z`** 0.0019 → 0.30 / 0.55 / 0.772 (arm angle 0.1° → 18.1° / 30.9° / 40.0°) → **6, 6, 6** (no change). The glyph stays an X in every one | **MEASURED-rev58** |

| **F66** | **`audit_brief.py --fix-count` CORRUPTED THE BRIEF IT EXISTS TO REPAIR, AND THEN REPORTED THE ROW AS PASSING.** Two scripts here print `ALL n PASS` — `verify_clone.sh` (258) and `bootstrap.sh` (10) — and the brief quotes **bootstrap's first**. The tool took the FIRST match as the stated count, read **10**, then blind-replaced every occurrence of the string `ALL 10 PASS`: it rewrote **all three** of the brief's bootstrap references to 258 and left `verify_clone`'s own two at 257. Its own row then went **green**, because the text it had just corrupted matched | **CLOSED-rev58.** The count is bound to `verify_clone` **BY CONTEXT, not by position** (a line naming `verify_clone`, or the SELF-CONSISTENCY sentence; lines naming `bootstrap` are excluded), the rewrite is **line-targeted**, and if the attribution is ambiguous it **REFUSES rather than guessing** | **WATCHED, both ways.** Broken deliberately to 999 and repaired: verify's three figures go 999 → 258 while bootstrap's three stay at **10**. Before the fix the same operation printed `rewrote 10 -> 258` and left the brief wrong in five places. **A second pass was needed even after the first fix** — the SELF-CONSISTENCY sentence carries the number *without* the `ALL n PASS` wrapper and was still reading 999 while the two lines beside it were correct, which is §10.3's own trap firing inside the repair for it | **MEASURED-rev58** |

A soft gradient, so it reads quiet per pixel, but it is *the* cue that says "product render on white" | In the SIDE frame the ground is **bit-exact (255,255,255) at every pixel below the tyres** — 4 columns × 15 rows sampled, 255 throughout, **no shadow at all**. In the hero the deepest ground under the sill is **220 DN against open ground at 255** — a **14 % darkening that reaches zero within ~150 px**. `studio.py`'s own comment already concedes *"the catcher's shadow survives the alpha-over at a few code values"*. **The untried route is a shadow stage in `post.py` driven off the catcher's alpha, which the rev-17 matte tap proved reaches 0.4980** — compositor-side, A/B-able, touches no geometry | **MEASURED-rev58** |
| **F68** | **`T.cylinder` CENTRES ON ITS ORIGIN AND FOUR CALL SITES AUTHOR IT AS A BASE.** `T.cylinder(center, axis, r, length)` → `solid_prism` → `_frame`, whose loop is `for s in (-depth / 2, depth / 2):` — **the solid straddles the point given.** Four squeeze-bottle/cap sites treat it as a base | **OPEN — the cheapest high-certainty fix on the register.** Predicted from source and then CONFIRMED IN PIXELS, which is why it is graded MEASURED and not INHERITED | Tail-run bottles: body spans 1.1865..1.3215 so its lower **67.5 mm is buried inside the 107 mm counter slab** (measured 70 mm); cap spans 1.3760..1.4020 leaving **54.5 mm of empty air between cap and bottle** (measured 49 mm). Warmer bottle: **20.5 mm inside the warmer**, cap **15.0 mm above** it. They float against white sky in the hero with no shadow and no support — **a floating object is the loudest possible tell per pixel.** The author corrects for this convention elsewhere (`(POST_X, s * POST_Y, _POST_Z_BOT + POST_LEN / 2.0)`), so it is a slip, not a misunderstanding. **Audit EVERY `_gcyl`/`T.cylinder` site in the same edit** — `gal_rail`, `gal_hook`, `gal_wrap`, `gal_warmer_tap` are all base-or-centre ambiguous and were NOT checked | **MEASURED-rev58** |
| **F69** | **THE HUBCAP VW IS THE SAME X AS THE NOSE ROUNDEL — ON FOUR WHEELS — AND F63's FIX WILL MISS IT IF IT LANDS IN THE WRONG PLACE.** Both badges run through the same generator: `glyph = T.vw_bars(1.0, CAP_EMBLEM_WFRAC, ...)` then `_fit_glyph(glyph, CAP_EMBLEM_D / 2, ax=('x', 'z'))` | **OPEN — and it CHANGES ITEM A's TARGET.** The rev-59 plan is *"putting `cream_cells` into `err()`"* in `probe_rev46_vw.py`, **which reads the NOSE only**, and `verify.py` says of the other badge *"hubcap badge is SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY"*. **If the re-solve lands on `vw_logo_fit` rather than on `vw_bars`/`V_SPINE`, four hubcaps keep the X** | **THE SIGN IS INVERTED BETWEEN THE TWO BADGES, which is the tell that the shared spine is the fault:** on the NOSE the **W's outer arms** float (0.6638 against a 0.7988 band). On the HUBCAP `t1_detail.cap_emblem`'s own docstring records the **V** falling short — *"the V's arm tips reach only 0.031069, i.e. 0.7154 of the fit radius, while the band's inner edge is at 0.8140. The V therefore stops 4.28 mm short"*. Four instances, ~30 px across at 1600 and ~70 px at 3840, dead centre of the four brightest circular objects in the frame | **MEASURED-rev58** |

Cause: `GAL_STEEL` at `rough=0.44, metal=1.0` under a white 13 × 8.5 m softbox returns the softbox, which is the body's value; and `gal_caddy` has **no dark frame member at all** | **MEASURED-rev58** |

The other runs down-and-AFT to (1384, 567), **82.8 px = 0.305 m**, into empty white. **The tail board has ZERO rows in either verifier (F23), so nothing will ever catch this** | **MEASURED-rev58** |

**RETRACTED BY THE SWEEP BEFORE PUBLISHING — recorded so nobody re-opens them:** *"the hubcap emblem is off-centre on the dome"* (dome parallax; centres agree within 2 px face-on); *"the front indicator is ~3.5 % too small"* (indicator/headlamp width ratio **0.48 render vs 0.475 photograph** — the size is RIGHT); *"the red's chip/peel is a measles field"* (**11.3 k specks/Mpx render against 8.9–10.4 k/Mpx in two photographs** — comparable; *residual risk: the render's specks are world-locked and grow 2.4× in pixels at 3840 while photographic grain does not, so re-measure on the first 3840 strip*); *"the two-tone break is missing a pinstripe"* (a pressed crease catching a highlight, not a stripe); *"the drip-rail bulbs are unlit"* (they are lit and warm — brightest 400 rail px read (246, 241, 235)).


## Added at rev 58 — the NOSE audit (owner-requested)

*Every ratio below was first run on `out/r58b_hero.png` as a CONTROL, where the
true value is computable from the source constants. A ratio whose render control
missed the source truth by more than a few percent is graded LEAD, not FINDING.*

| ID | finding | status | the number | grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **F75** | **THE NOSE TWO-TONE BREAK PASSES FAR TOO CLOSE ABOVE THE HEADLAMP AND THE INDICATOR.** Ruler = the headlamp's own VERTICAL radius, so numerator and denominator are verticals at the same depth on the same panel and **yaw *and* pitch cancel** | **OPEN — rank 1 on the nose, and the most strongly evidenced finding in this audit.** No single constant fixes it: inverting each candidate alone gives `V_POW` 0.345 vs 0.214, `V_APEX` 0.745 vs 0.949 (both above the bumper crown 0.5360, which would expose the wedge apex — refuted), `HL_DROP` fits both by construction but pushes lamp-to-belt from the photographed **0.339 ± 0.025 m** to 0.391 (2σ conflict with the arm that justified it), `V_HALF_W` 0.708 vs 0.736. **It is a re-solve of the V against TWO constraints — the same shape as F65** | break above **lamp** centre, in lamp-radii: model **1.280**, render control **1.343 (+4.9 %)**, `ref_nolita_front34` **2.121**, `..._front34b` **2.100**, `ref_playa_34` **1.951**, `ref_workshop` (bare aperture, no chrome, no bloom) **2.127**. Above **indicator** centre: model **0.153**, photographs **0.875 / 0.880 / 0.805 / 0.803**. **Four frames, two vehicles, three liveries, two independent features, all agreeing.** Magnitude **52 mm** on the unambiguous bare-aperture ruler (lamp and indicator agreeing to 1 mm), **73–80 mm** on the red-bus frames whose ruler is the chrome rim — the model's rim stands 16.5 mm outside its own bore and **no frame we hold shows a rim and its aperture together**, so that 1.19 conversion cannot be checked. **Honest range 50–80 mm, best single estimate 52 mm** — **⚠ AND AT REV 61 AN ADVERSARY NAMED THE PROBLEM WITH THAT 52: it is the BARE-APERTURE reading, which comes from `ref_workshop`, the GREEN vehicle — and `probe_rev59_nose.py` prints EVERY RUN that `ref_workshop` is EXCLUDED because paint does not transfer (rule 11). A two-tone break IS paint, and the rev-61 corollary that the roundel's SHAPE is a pressing does not rescue it. So the 52 mm is quoted from a frame this project's own instrument refuses. The 73–80 mm rim-ruled figure does not have that problem. UNRESOLVED — and note `ref_workshop`'s 2.127 is on the SAME ruler as M1's lens-ruled 2.115, which nothing has used.** | **MEASURED-rev58** |
| **F76** | **AND F75 CLOSES AN OPEN OWNER QUESTION — DO NOT RE-ASK IT.** `probe_rev44_nolita_nose.py`'s docstring says *"Whether the Nolita paint follows the PRESSED SWAGE ... IS NOT KNOWN AND IS NOW AN OWNER QUESTION."* | **CLOSED-rev58** | Three independent paint jobs on two different vehicles put the break in the same place to **±5 %** (2.13 / 2.12 / 2.10 / 1.95 lamp-radii). **It follows the pressing.** | **MEASURED-rev58** |
| **F77** | **`V_POW = 0.60` IS TOO LARGE; TWO FRAMES READ ≈ 0.52** — and a `verify_clone.sh` self-consistency row pins the contradicted value in THREE places | **OPEN.** `ck "V_POW is 0.60"`, `ck "V_POW_Z is 0.60"` and `ck "V_POW and V_POW_Z agree"` must be re-based **together**, with the cause named — never relax one copy (§10.8). `verify.py`'s `V_POW >= 1.0` guard is unaffected | The arm is `z = V_APEX + V_RISE·(|y|/V_HALF_W)^p`; under yaw, with the arm normalised to its own endpoints, **`p` is pose-invariant**. Fitted in image space, apex free: **render control 0.605 / 0.620** against a source truth of **0.600** — the method recovers it to ±0.02. Photographs: `ref_nolita_front34` **0.517** (rms 1.88 px, n=121) and **0.521** (tighter window, rms 1.39), `ref_workshop` **0.531**. Effect at the lamp station **+24.5 mm** — roughly HALF of F75's 52 mm | **MEASURED-rev58** |
| **F78** | **`IND_DZ = 0.2060` IS CONTRADICTED BY THE VERY FRAME ITS OWN COMMENT CITES.** grep `IND_DZ  = 0.2060                 # measured above the lamp, ref_workshop.jpg` | **OPEN — cheap, but fix F75's instrument first or the same millimetres get chased twice** | Indicator above lamp, in lamp-radii: model 2.390 (aperture) / 2.006 (bezel), **render control 2.021 (+0.7 %, the best control in this audit)**, `ref_nolita_front34` 1.845, `...34b` 1.800, `ref_playa_34` 1.707, **`ref_workshop` (both bare holes, no pod parallax) 1.859 against the model's 2.390**. Re-measured: **IND_DZ ≈ 0.160 m** (workshop) or 0.183 (red bus) against a built 0.206 ± 0.010 — contradicted at **≥ 2.3σ, and at 4.6σ by its own cited photograph** | **MEASURED-rev58** |
| **F79** | **THE NOSE ROUNDEL IS 10–24 % TOO LARGE relative to the headlamp.** Adjacent to F63 but NOT the same finding — F63 is the glyph's shape INSIDE the ring; this is the ring's DIAMETER | **OPEN — must be coordinated with F63/F69, not acted on alone**: moving `ROUNDEL_D` moves the glyph's fit radius, which is F38's point too | roundel vertical D ÷ headlamp vertical D: model 1.363, render control 1.443 (+5.9 %), `ref_nolita_front34` **1.283**; in aperture terms model 1.624 against `ref_workshop`'s **1.239 (−24 %)**. `ref_workshop` implies **`ROUNDEL_D` ≈ 0.214 m** against the built **0.2800** | **MEASURED-rev58** |
| **F80** | **THE HEADLAMP RENDERS AS A DARK HOLE; IN THE PHOTOGRAPHS IT IS BRIGHTER THAN THE PAINT AROUND IT.** The mechanism was written down in the source and predicted there: `headlamp_recess_cutters.__doc__` — *"a mirror in an unlit cavity returns the cavity"* | **OPEN — and it is ALREADY GATED BY SOMETHING NOTHING RUNS.** `probe_rev45_nose.py`'s N2/C4/C6 gate this against `ref_playa_34` at 0.565 lens/cream — a different normaliser from this audit's. **Run it before touching anything** | lens interior mean luminance ÷ adjacent body-red mean, same frame (exposure-free): **render 0.884 — the lens is DARKER than the paint**; `ref_playa_34` (lamp off) **1.603**; `ref_nolita_front34b` (lamp off, in shade) **1.268** | **MEASURED-rev58** |

**NOSE LEADS, not findings:** **L1** the lamp sits ~130–160 mm too high above the bumper (direction consistent across three frames, but the weakest control in the set and only one frame gives a clean column; **could not decide whether the lamp is too high or the bumper too low** — there is no controlled absolute vertical scale at the nose). **L2** the nose dome is `bulge = 0.019 * w * ...`, 19 mm of crown over a ~1.6 m panel, and the render shades as a plane where every photograph shows a strongly barrelled pressing. **L3** the pressed V swage (`step = -0.0062 * w * (1.0 - s)`, a 6.2 mm step smeared over ~32 mm) produces **no luminance ridge at all** in the render — the two-tone reads as a decal, not a pressing. Ungated. **L4** `IND_DY` unresolved: the red-bus frames AGREE with the render (1.00/1.11/1.05 vs 1.084) while `ref_workshop`'s parallax-free hole says 38 mm too far outboard — **the two evidence classes disagree and no change is proposed.**

**NOSE NEGATIVE RESULTS (expected wrong, measured RIGHT):** the two-tone TOPOLOGY is correct and the arms land on the belt at the front corner (verified in both render and photograph to ~1 px); the indicator POD SIZE is right (0.385 model / 0.383 render / 0.341–0.408 photographs); the bumper blade section is right; and **`HL_Z`'s belt arm still checks out** — `Z_BELT_AUTH − HL_Z = 0.3390`, exactly the photographed 0.339 ± 0.025 — *which is why F75 cannot be fixed by simply dropping the lamps again.*

**THE INSTRUMENT TO BUILD FIRST, AND IT IS ONE RENDER.** `studio.py` already carries an orthographic FRONT elevation — `"front":    dict(loc=(26.0, 0.0, 1.52), tgt=(0.0, 0.0, 1.52), lens=None, ortho=3.55)`, reachable as `T1_PREVIEW=front`. **An orthographic front render removes perspective and plan-curvature bias entirely**, letting every ratio above be read against the constants with no camera solve. **Nothing in this tree appears to have ever used it on the nose.** The horizontal-ratio bias it removes is proven: the same render gives u_lamp **0.400** measured against the arm's endpoints (truth 0.634) but **2.658** against the bezel (truth 2.653).

| **F151** | **C8's PHOTOGRAPH TARGET HAS A SILENT FAILURE MODE, AND NOTHING IN THE SHIPPED PROBE SWEEPS IT.** `cell_elongation()` takes its measuring region as an ellipse inscribed in **THE MASK ARRAY'S RECTANGLE** at frac 0.97 — `n0, n1 = mask.shape` — **not in the measured badge**. The shipped crop (`ref_nolita_front34.jpg` rows 192–260, cols 153–193) happens to be tight, so it works. Widen it by 3 px a side and the disc escapes the roundel: the cream nose OUTSIDE the ring becomes a **479 px** "cream cell" against the true cells' 215, **39 % of it outside the ring's own bounding box**, and the target reads **1.553** — indistinguishable from the built glyph's 1.49. **At that window C8 reports the owner's top defect CLOSED and no control fires.** C1 sweeps six thresholds × five windows for L1–L6; this target is swept by nothing. Watched failing as C11, and the mechanism watched as C12 | **OPEN — THE VERDICT SURVIVES, THE CEILING DOES NOT.** Within ±2 px the target reads **3.390 / 3.188 / 2.950**, and the segmentation sweep (R > 90..140, G,B < 0.50..0.70 R) gives **2.969 .. 3.415**. So the built glyph is **1.99× .. 2.27× too round — a RANGE, not the point 2.27× four documents quote**. The fix is to derive the disc from the BADGE's own fitted ellipse rather than from the crop rectangle; not done here, and it must be watched failing on the escape case before it is believed. **Also: `probe_rev46_vw.py`'s own header still says *"photograph 3.33"* where the probe prints 3.39** | **MEASURED-rev62** |
| **F152** | **L6 IS NOT THE ANSWER TO "WHICH OF L1–L6 IS WRONG" — REFUTED BY ABLATION, NOT BY ARGUMENT.** The cross-frame route (F153) makes **L6, the stroke width, the largest disagreement**, and the workshop badge says the strokes are thinner. **F102 had swept `T1_VW_WFRAC` and called it inert — but that was against C6, THE CELL COUNT. C8 did not exist until rev 61**, so the lever for the accused landmark had never been tested against the statistic that measures the defect. Rule 36 | **REFUTED-rev62.** Ablated across wfrac 0.08..0.44: C8 moves **1.07 .. 1.82**, and it moves the **WRONG WAY** — thicker strokes give ROUNDER cells, so F102's "inert" verdict was about the count only. Thinning to the construction's limit reaches **1.82 against the photograph's 3.39 — 54 % of the way — and costs a cell** (6 → 5 at wfrac ≤ 0.10). **Abandoning the stroke-width landmark ENTIRELY still cannot reach the target.** This is consistent with F137's ceiling of 1.634 subject to the landmark bar, and extends it: the bar is not what is holding elongation down. **AND C8's CLAIMED SCALE-STABILITY DOES NOT HOLD ACROSS THE SWEEP** — at wfrac 0.12 it reads **1.49 at 276 rows against 1.88 at 69**. The stability C8 advertises is a property of the shipped point, not of the statistic **SUPERSEDED AT REV 66 (rule 13): F152 tested THINNING, against C8's target of 3.390 — which F194 re-based to 2.63–2.96 — and from inside a landmark residual F203 shows was 96 % one broken row. Measured the other way at rev 66, THICKENING moves elongation TOWARD the target and puts L6 ON the photograph: `wfrac` 0.1800 → 0.2283, SHIPPED (F204). The row is kept, not deleted — its warning against reaching the target BY THINNING still stands, and it is now also the textbook case for rule 46: a refutation inherits its instrument.** | **MEASURED-rev62** |
| **F153** | **THE WORKSHOP BADGE DOES NOT DECIDE WHICH LANDMARK IS WRONG — and L1 is CORROBORATED.** The rev-62 brief's ranked item 1 prescribes re-deriving L1–L6 on `ref_workshop.jpg`'s badge (1.71× the area) under F141's pressing-is-geometry corollary. Taken, with `probe_rev46_vw.py`'s own `landmarks()` lifted by `ast` so the two rulers cannot drift (C0w agrees to 0.00003). Raw deltas nolita → workshop: **L1 +0.0016, L2 −0.0172, L3 +0.0333, L4 −0.0451, L5 +0.0324, L6 −0.0324** | **CEILED-rev62 — "it cannot be recovered from what we hold."** Resampling the workshop badge down to the nolita badge's raster scale moves L6 **0.1204 → 0.132 ± 0.013**, so **roughly a third of the largest disagreement is the raster-scale confound, not the badge**; at matched scale no landmark's gap clears its own combined spread by more than ~1.5 σ. **L1 is the real result: 0.1940 vs 0.1957 across two vehicles, two segmentations and 1.71× the area.** This is the SECOND independent route to the emblem's angular/positional arrangement that this frame has failed to settle — F141 was the first, and both failed on the same underlying cause: **the pressing is chrome, and chrome has no flat tone to threshold.** **AND MY FIRST MASK WAS WRONG, CAUGHT BY PAINTING IT (rule 4):** at th = 118 it took the LEFT FLANK OF EVERY STROKE and nothing else — the shadow flank, with the specular flank dropped — which is **F08's recorded failure mode arriving on a new statistic**. It produced six plausible deltas (L3 −0.3128, L2 −0.2554) and every one was an artefact. **Two of my own controls were also wrong**: C2w compared crop margins rather than badges, and C3w was aimed at the workshop WALL rather than the vehicle. Rewritten as an equivalence test — an `S` against the explicit `C` that is the same curve, which must agree pixel for pixel — with its own kill. **That is the second wrong instrument rev 63 caught in its own work; the first was C24 claiming "AT AN IDENTICAL RASTER" while printing a 276-row figure against the photograph's 41 × 69 (rule 38), caught by reading the row against its own claim** | **OPEN as a standing instrument.** `CLAUDE.md` rule 4: *"every recent revision has caught four to seven of its OWN instruments being wrong"*. The queue was killed after saving `front` and `side`, at sample 33/96 of `hero`, **by this session's worker process being restarted, not by anything in the tree**: no OOM (15 GB free at the time), no Blender error, no traceback, and the process was simply gone. (2) Its first KILL collapsed the W's arms onto the axis and demanded the contact count DROP; it went **4 → 5**, and the CONTROL was the defect — **`_on_band` projects every terminal radially onto the band circle by construction, so moving a spine point inboard changes its ANGLE, not its reach.** Replaced with a synthetic kill on six radial bars whose answer is known by construction: reaching reads **6**, retracted to 0.72 reads **0** | **CLOSED — both repaired, both repairs watched.** Four of rev 63's own instruments have now been wrong (with F172's two), every one producing a plausible number first. `CLAUDE.md` rule 4 budgets for exactly this | **MEASURED-rev63** |

## THE OWNER'S STANDING BARS — RECOVERED FROM A STRANDED BRANCH AT REV 58b

**RULE 16, AND IT NEARLY WENT WRONG AGAIN.** These three are the owner's OWN
STANDING INSTRUCTIONS. They were restored to the register by the rev-57b drift
audit on `origin/claude/bus-model-rev57-yvrlhi` — a branch that was never merged
— and they appear **ZERO times** in this branch's live register or brief
(grepped, both files, at rev 58b). They went with the rev-44 carrier deletion.

**THEY KEEP THAT BRANCH'S IDs — F61, F63, F64 THERE — AND ARE DELIBERATELY NOT
RENUMBERED HERE.** This branch already uses F61/F63/F64 for different findings.
That collision is an open owner decision (§0.0) and resolving it is not this
block's job; **losing the bars a second time while waiting for that decision
is the thing this block exists to prevent.**

**RESOLVED AT REV 59.** The owner ruled *"merge it, renumber its IDs"*. The branch is merged and
its findings are F88–F97; these three bars now carry their permanent IDs **F91**, **F93** and
**F94**, and the full rows are in the merged block above. **The bars themselves are unchanged and
still OPEN** — merging the branch did not satisfy any of them.

## Added at rev 59 — the cab door, the front arch, and two instruments that disagreed

*Everything below was measured by `probe_rev59_door.py`, whose five controls run the
identical pixel code on the side render — where the front arch IS a circle of `ARCH_R`
about `X_AXLE_F` by construction — and recover `ARCH_R` to −0.05 %, the arch centre to
0.17 px, and the built lobes end to end to −0.65 % / +2.48 %. Run it as*
`python3 probe_rev59_door.py out/<pfx>_side.png` *— it says* **NO RENDER** *and exits 2
if the frame is absent (rule 37), and it takes no revision-numbered default.*

| ID | finding | status | figure | grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **F81** | **THE CAB DOOR'S FORWARD LOBE STOOD 67 mm TOO FAR FORWARD.** Two stale constants compounded: `DOOR_LOBE_A/B` were built off the WHEEL HUB column while the datum they are used against is `_ARCH_CX`, the ARCH's centre (**9.83 px apart** in `ref_nolita_doorshut.jpg` — rule 34 exactly), and off `39.54 px`, which is `ARCH_R × 105.9`, a scale obtained by ASSUMING the radius it then measures | **CLOSED-rev59.** Both re-derived in the flank plane, so the ratio carries no px/m and no parallax term. Built now **0.7098 / 0.9600** against the photograph's **0.7096 / 0.9598** — 0.1 mm. `VERIFY: 0 fail, 0 warn` and the shut line 100 % open at `T1_SUB=1` **and** `T1_SUB=2`. Ablation `T1_DOOR_STALE=1`, **watched failing** | photograph `DOOR_LOBE_A` **0.7096**, `DOOR_LOBE_B` **0.9598** against a built 0.8877 / 1.1406 — **aft by 66.5 and 67.5 mm**. The two feet move by the same amount and the ramp's WIDTH comes back at **0.2502 of R against the built 0.2529, −1.07 %**: the step was the right SIZE in the wrong PLACE | **MEASURED-rev59** |
| **F82** | **"THE FRONT ARCH IS UP TO 0.13 A ≈ 48 mm INBOARD OF A CIRCLE" IS REFUTED — IT IS A METHOD ARTEFACT.** The rev-59 brief §3.10 swept radius-versus-angle about a GUESSED centre (crown column, hub row). Run unchanged on the side render, whose arch is exactly circular, that sweep invents swings of **±0.07 R** and misstates R by **+17 %**; §3.10's 1.0241 → 0.8723 fall sits inside its own artefact band. The crown COLUMN is the weak link — near the crown the arc is flat, so row noise maps to huge column error (**+5.5 to +26 px** on the render, where truth is 0) | **REFUTED-rev59.** A circle fit over a radius-scaled window replaces it and recovers the render's centre to **0.17 px** and R to **−0.05 %** | the sweep's own error is LARGER than the non-circularity it was used to claim | **MEASURED-rev59** |
| **F83** | **THE FRONT ARCH IS NEVERTHELESS NOT A CIRCLE — BY ABOUT 4.4 mm rms, NOT 48 mm.** And the half that matters cannot be recovered | **OPEN, CEILED, AND DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT.** `probe_rev59_door` M3 fails BY DESIGN and must keep failing until either the arch is rebuilt or the owner rules. Building it symmetric would be inventing the forward half: below the door line that half is deep shade with the white bumper cutting in, and §3.10 concedes the same. **A rebuild is not needed for F81** — the lobes moved aft with the arch left circular and both `T1_SUB` levels are clean | photograph **1.171 % of R rms** (n=54) against the render's **0.237 %** on a KNOWN circle — **5.0×** the instrument floor, so REAL; on `ARCH_R` that is **4.4 mm rms**, and below SPEC §2's ±8 mm tyre-gap lock | **MEASURED-rev59, CEILED** |
| **F84** | **THE CLEARANCE ASSERT FORBADE THE VEHICLE.** Both thresholds on the cab-door/front-arch clearance were REGRESSION BASELINES armed at rev 41's own accidental clearance, never at a measured one — and its inherited rationale (the 205562 v → 12 v collapse) is already refuted in this repo by **SPEC §10.62**: *"That does not transfer: all six crossings were live at SUB=2 with zero non-manifold edges"* | **CLOSED-rev59** by a re-base with the cause named. The companion test is NOT a second proxy: `verify.py` already tests the collapse DIRECTLY — *no boolean rolled back*, and the shell's non-manifold edge count — and both pass at both levels. `DOOR_ARCH_G` is kept and REPORTED as the historical anchor rather than enforced as a bar | rev 41 demanded **0.0653 of `ARCH_R`**; the photographed vehicle clears its own arch by **0.0226 of `ARCH_R` = 8.4 mm**, about a THIRD. Built now 0.0177 R = 6.6 mm | **MEASURED-rev59** |
| **F85** | **`gloss_compare.py` REWRITES A TRACKED FILE AS A SIDE EFFECT.** Running the gate dirties the working tree | **CLOSED-rev60.** The painted tiles go to `out/` now; `T1_GL_TILES=track` restores the old destination. Watched: running the gate leaves the tree clean. Harmless to the measurement, but `verify_clone.sh`'s *"modified tracked files"* row fires afterwards, and a context that runs the gate then verifies will hunt a change it did not make. It cost this revision one such hunt | `probe_scratch/rev57_gloss_render.png`, 1001961 → 1008539 bytes | **MEASURED-rev59** |
| **F86** | **THE TWO NOSE INSTRUMENTS DISAGREE, AND THE REASON IS THE RED/CREAM LEVEL — NOT THE LAMP. THIS RE-FRAMES F80.** `probe_rev45_nose.py` C4 normalises the lens by CREAM and **PASSES**; F80 normalises it by BODY RED and reports a large gap. Both were run this revision; all three patches were then measured in ONE frame each, with the windows PAINTED AND LOOKED AT — and the first two cream windows were wrong (one on the roundel's strokes, sd 57.7; one straddling the V-arm, sd 45.8), which is rule 8 firing on its own author | **OPEN — a LEAD, not a finding, and not attributed to a cause (rule 29.3).** Its CEILING is stated: the two frames are a sunlit exterior and a cyclorama, so the ratios are exposure-free only under linear response and uniform illumination across the three patches, which does not hold between them. What it does say is that **the lamp is nearly right against cream and the next revision should not assume F80 is the lamp** | lens/cream render **0.636** vs photo **0.688** (−8 %); lens/red render **1.292** vs photo **1.973** (−35 %); **red/cream render 0.493 vs photo 0.349 — the render's red is 1.41× brighter relative to its cream** | **MEASURED-rev59** |
| **F87** | **THE ORTHOGRAPHIC FRONT ELEVATION HAS NOW BEEN POINTED AT THE NOSE, AND IT CONFIRMS F75 POSE-FREE.** `T1_PREVIEW=front` existed and, as the rev-59 brief says, nothing in this tree had ever used it for this. Being orthographic it removes perspective and plan-curvature bias entirely, so it needs no camera recovery and no `flank_kv` — F26's camera ambiguity never arises | **OPEN — F75 stands, now on a pose-free instrument.** The two lamps agree to 4 % and their midpoint lands on the frame's own centre column to **0.25 px**, which is the projection's own control | two-tone break above the lamp centre, in that lamp's own vertical radii: **1.161 and 1.206** (left, right) against the photographs' **1.951–2.127**. To reach 2.12 the break must rise **33 px = 73 mm** — inside §3.11's honest 50–80 mm range, at the top of it | **MEASURED-rev59** |

the render at **3200×2200** (roundel 241 px) reads **6/5/2** — **at the lowest threshold the render MATCHES the photograph's six.** Corroborated three ways on the mesh: C6's built raster makes 6, `vw_ring`'s inner edge measures **0.11199 m** against the glyph's max radius **0.11788 m** (a **5.9 mm overlap**, so the strokes reach the band by construction), and every angular bin that carries a terminal reaches **1.050–1.053** of the inner edge | **WHAT ACTUALLY REMAINS IS THRESHOLD-FRAGILITY FROM LOW INK CONTRAST, AND IT IS NOT THE SPINE.** Exposure-free, ink as a fraction of the cream it sits on: **photograph 0.409, render 0.587** — the render's mark is far lighter against its own cream, which is why its count degrades with threshold where the photograph's is flat. **The roundel is NOT self-shadowed** — disc/nose cream is 0.930 photographed against 0.918 rendered, i.e. the same. **The cause is the red's desaturation, which is already measured and already localised:** emblem RGB **(236, 20, 8)** photographed against **(121, 58, 40)** rendered, and the BODY red shows the identical shift — G/R 0.00 → 0.42 — which is `flank_compare`'s own live figure and **F128's finding that the excess is the SURROUND, not the paint** (photograph spread 0.001 across two scenes, render 0.133 across two views of one build) | **AND MY OWN ABLATION FOR IT WAS A TAUTOLOGY, CAUGHT BY RUNNING IT.** I raised the rendered roundel's SATURATION 1.0 → 3.0× and re-ran the count expecting it to climb to 6/6/6. It did not move: **6/5/2 → 6/5/1**, ink/cream 0.587 → 0.613. **`cream_cells` reads LUMA, and I scaled saturation AROUND luma — holding constant the very quantity the statistic reads.** Rule 6, in the ablation I wrote to test rule 6's shape. **CEILING ON THE HEADLINE: two resolutions, and only the thr-20 column moved (5 → 6); thr 30 still reads 5 at both.** So the honest statement is *"the count recovers at the lowest threshold when the roundel doubles in pixels"*, not *"F205 is closed"*. **WHAT IS NOT AFFECTED AND REMAINS THE EMBLEM'S LIVE GEOMETRY WORK: C4 at 0.0755 (bar 0.045) and C8's cell SHAPE — built elongation 2.55 against 3.39, the cells 1.33× too round.** A count and a shape are different statistics; this finding moves the count only | **MEASURED-rev69** |

Against a TWO-RENDER FLOOR of 0.001 on the spread (rule 49), 1600×1100 at 96 spp:**
`env 0 (shipped) + coat 0.02   spread 0.412  headroom 0.126`
`env 1.0         + coat 0.02   spread 0.416  headroom  --`
`env 21.0        + coat 0.02   spread 0.389  headroom 0.118`
`env 21.0        + coat 0.55   spread 0.399  headroom 0.181`
**THE COAT DOES MAKE SPECULAR STRUCTURE FOR THE FIRST TIME — headroom +44 %, which is 88x F54's "+0.5 % of gloss"** — and every configuration that raises the highlight also lifts the paint's floor, so the SPREAD falls. At roughness 0.250 the specular lobe is broad: **a bright environment veils before it glints.** And the coat's chroma cost is real and reproduces F54's number: **flank_compare's red G/R 0.422 -> 0.520**, where the photograph is 0.114. **RULE 44: the guard went red on my own new work and the guard WINS. `T1_REFLENV` ships at 0.0 and the coat at its shipped 0.02** | **WHAT IS ACTUALLY LEFT OF F62, AND IT IS NOT NOTHING.** Its ceiling STANDS, but it now stands on a measurement rather than on *"this flank's specular image is white cyclorama 19.3 m away"*. **The binding constraint is the ROUGHNESS, not the environment:** at 0.250 the lobe is too broad for any environment to glint through, so the next attempt must move roughness and environment TOGETHER — the one pairing that has still never been tried, and the switches for both now exist. **THE LEVEL WAS NOT TUNED EITHER:** 21.0 is `studio.py`'s own recorded paper white (*"the studio's paper white sits at linear 21.0 to reach display 253"*), carried from the file, which is why the first attempt at strength 1.0 was invisible — reflections at 1/21 of white, and the gate moved 0.412 -> 0.416, real but 150x too small | **MEASURED-rev70** |

It is IDENTICAL across all six renders, so the COMPARISONS hold; the absolute value should be quoted from flank_compare** | **MEASURED-rev70** |

Inside the 0.59 bound`. **550 mm on a 1750 mm body is 31 % of the width, and nothing has ever measured it.** It is the largest of the three and the only one with no number of any kind | **WHAT THE FRAMES SHOW, LOOKED AT AT REV 69 RATHER THAN TRANSCRIBED (rule 1).** `IMG_3840.jpeg` cropped to (355, 85)–(480, 215) shows the hatch on the **CHALKBOARD** vehicle: a panel with **visible EDGE THICKNESS**, an **orange top face**, a **dark angled recess cut into its forward-lower area**, emerging **from behind the rounded rear roof dome**. `ref_rear34.jpg` cropped to (820, 0)–(1200, 300) shows the same panel **on the RED bus**: a **CREAM face, a RED edge stripe along its raised edge, and a string of lit BULBS along that edge.** **SO THE COLOUR IS ALREADY ROUGHLY RIGHT AND THE DEFECT IS FORM** — which is F163's own correction, and **no brief has ever carried it forward**, so every revision since rev 62 has inherited the impression that the colour is open. **DO NOT paint it orange: that face belongs to `IMG_3840.jpeg`'s chalkboard livery and rule 11 forbids transferring paint between livery states of the same vehicle** | **AND ONE THING SEEN AND DELIBERATELY NOT NAMED (rule 37).** In `out/r69_hero34r.png` a **large dark rectangle** sits below the blade with red visible through it. **Rev 69 did NOT establish whether that is `glass_rear` rendering black — which would be F71's flat glazing, a DIFFERENT finding — or an actual hole in the shell.** `STATE.md`'s *"roof aperture: open, and solid fore / aft / both sides"* is about a **different aperture** and must not be quoted at it. **Measure it before naming it.** Note `lid_trunk` is a **different object** (a plate at x −1.87, z 0.608…1.103, below the counter) and is **NOT** this hatch. **AND THE CEILING ON F165 IS BINDING AND MUST TRAVEL WITH ITS NUMBERS: the frame is 480×320 and the hatch spans ~90 px — TWO SIGNIFICANT FIGURES. Do not fit to 28.0 as though it were 28.00** | **MEASURED-rev69** |
| **F242** | **THE REAR HATCH'S ANGLE IS A POSE, NOT A DIMENSION — F165's 28.0° IS REFUTED, AND THE SHIPPED 38.0° WAS RIGHT ALL ALONG.** Rev 70's brief made *"38.0° against a photographed 28.0°, ~10° too steep"* its **#1 item**, on the owner's own words. **Re-measured on BOTH frames, every pick PAINTED before the arithmetic:** `ref_side.jpg` — the RED bus in its **CURRENT** livery, F163's own **PRIMARY** frame — images the board at **38.8°** (ends picked and painted at `probe_scratch/rev70_hatch_ends.png`, base (876, 294) → tip (1014, 183), a 177 px baseline); `IMG_3840.jpeg` images the **SAME BOARD** at **21.0°** (the orange top face's CENTRELINE fitted over 60 columns — the two long edges of a rectangle CONVERGE under perspective so the centreline is the chord's estimator — rms 2.35 px, bracket 19.6–22.4). **THE TWO FRAMES DISAGREE BY 18° BECAUSE THE BOARD IS HINGED AND SOMEBODY PROPPED IT DIFFERENTLY ON TWO DIFFERENT DAYS.** | **AND F165's 28.0 MATCHES NEITHER FRAME — IT SITS BETWEEN THEM.** F165 measured one frame and recorded the result as though the angle were a property of the vehicle. **The shipped 38.0 matches the primary frame to 0.8°, so building to 28.0 — which is what the brief instructed — would have made the model WORSE against the very vehicle being recreated.** This is **rule 35** (a guard written against a POSE encodes that pose) and **rule 11** (which frame, which livery state) firing together on the project's top item. **THE CONTROL THAT MAKES THE IMAGE-PLANE ANGLES USABLE:** the front and rear hub centres are level in the vehicle frame and image at **−0.56°**, so horizontal images as horizontal in these frames to ~1° — without it an image-plane angle is not a vehicle angle at all | **AND RULE 8 KILLED THREE WINDOWS ON THE WAY, EVERY ONE CAUGHT BY PAINTING AND NONE BY REASONING.** A "cream face" mask on `ref_side.jpg` selected **the pale WALL behind the bus** and read 33.5°; a "bulb string" mask selected **the red wall graphics** across the whole crop and read 24.7° at an rms of 27.6 px on a 174 px baseline; and the orange face's **principal axis** read 21.7° because a foreshortened wedge's principal axis is not its chord. **Three plausible numbers, all published-ready, all wrong.** `T1_TB_TILT` is the new ablation, and **the guard's angle arm was WATCHED FIRING at exactly 28.0** so the next context can see the refutation rather than take it on trust | **MEASURED-rev70** |
| **F243** | **THE REAR HATCH'S REAL DEFECT IS ITS LENGTH, AND TWO INDEPENDENT FRAMES AGREE: 0.7110 m SHIPPED AGAINST ≥0.82 MEASURED — ~14 % SHORT SINCE REV 49.** `ref_side.jpg`, independent of F165 in frame, ruler and pick: the board images **177.1 px** between two painted ends at `t1_shell.py`'s own recorded **k_t = 215.5 ± 3.0 px/m** for that frame → **0.822 ± 0.011 m**. `IMG_3840.jpeg` (F165, rev 62, landmarks painted) → **≥0.829 m**. **They agree to 0.9 % across two frames, two scales and eight revisions.** **`TB_CHORD` 0.7110 → 0.8250**, the mean of the two bounds | **AND THE SHIPPED VALUE IS ITSELF A LOWER BOUND, STATED AS ONE (rule 12).** Both reads understate: the board sits **INBOARD of the flank plane**, where px/m is smaller and the metric length correspondingly larger. 0.8250 is therefore **the shortest length consistent with both frames, not an estimate of the true one**, which is ≥ it. Measured on the mesh after the change: principal-axis chord **0.8250 m**, tip moves x −2.4131 → **−2.5031**, z 2.2003 → **2.2703**. Everything downstream scales off the constant — the edge bands, the stay triangle and the bulb count all follow, so no second literal went stale | **THE GATE THIS OBJECT HAS NEVER HAD, IN THE SAME EDIT (rule 13), AND BOTH ARMS WATCHED FAILING (rule 3).** `verify._tail_board_pose` reads the board's **PRINCIPAL AXIS in XZ off the built mesh** and **names that ruler in its own text** — because **F165's published "built 38.4° / 0.732 m" is the BOUNDING-BOX DIAGONAL** (`atan(0.455/0.574)`, `hypot(0.574, 0.455)`), which carries the board's thickness and its rounded corners, while the constants are 38.0 / 0.7110. Comparing a bbox diagonal against a photographed edge is how a bookkeeping difference got published as a measurement of the vehicle (rule 38). **KILLS: `T1_TB_CHORD=0.7110` → `VERIFY: 1 fail`, "TOO SHORT"; `T1_TB_TILT=28.0` → `VERIFY: 1 fail`, the angle arm.** The principal-axis ruler recovers 0.8250 / 38.0 exactly, where the bbox diagonal would read 0.73 / 38.4 | **MEASURED-rev70** |

It reads black because a Transmission-1.00 pane looks into an **unlit interior**, not because anything is missing. **This CLOSES the question rev 69 handed forward and hands the object to F71**, where the live defect is that the glazing is a flat slab (0.5 % sd against the photograph's 12.8 %) | **AND ONE THING THAT CANNOT BE RECOVERED, STATED AS A RESULT RATHER THAN BUILT ON A GUESS (rule 12).** F163 lists a **"dark angled recess cut into its forward-lower area"** as one of the things to build. It is visible on `IMG_3840.jpeg` at ~0.42 of the visible chord — **but that frame is the CHALKBOARD livery, the feature sits on a surface `ref_side.jpg` does not show, and at 480 px it cannot be separated into geometry, paint or shadow.** The PRIMARY frame shows a **clean cream face with no recess**. **Rule 11 transfers geometry and not paint, and this cannot be classified as either — so it was NOT built.** *"It cannot be recovered from what we hold"* is the result | **MEASURED-rev70** |
| **F245** | **I SHIPPED A REGRESSION ON THE OWNER'S TOP ITEM AND A DISPATCHED ADVERSARY CAUGHT IT. `TB_CHORD` 0.7110 → 0.8250 IS REVERTED.** The reasoning felt decisive and was wrong three ways. **(1) WRONG RULER (rule 38).** I read the board's two painted ends on `ref_side.jpg` — 177.1 px — and divided by the flat `k_t = 215.5 px/m` this file records. But `ref_side.jpg` is a **PROJECTIVE** image of the flank, and `flank_compare.py` says so in its own header: *"every scalar px/m is wrong somewhere. Two instruments exist and this file uses them instead of REF_PPM."* The board sits **130–265 columns AFT** of the hub that scalar was taken at. Through the project's own `flank_X` (SPEC 10.35) and `flank_kv` (SPEC 10.34), **the SAME two picks give 0.7899 m, not 0.822.** **(2) THE FILE ALREADY SAID SO, TWO LINES BELOW THE CONSTANT:** *"reading it with a single px/m **over-reads by 4.8 %**"* — and I adopted a value LARGER than the over-read | **(3) AND IT BROKE A MEASURED CLOSURE THAT WAS STILL PRINTED IN THE SOURCE AS CLOSING — THIS IS THE DECISIVE ONE.** The board's base and tip heights are **INDEPENDENTLY MEASURED**: z **1.7470 ± 0.027** and z **2.184 ± 0.030**. `(2.184 − 1.747)/sin(38°) = 0.7098 m`. **THE 0.7110 THIS CONSTANT HAS CARRIED SINCE REV 49 IS NOT A PIXEL READ AT ALL — IT IS DERIVED FROM TWO MEASURED HEIGHTS**, and I replaced a two-height closure with a single flat-scalar pixel read without noticing the derivation was there. 0.8250 puts the tip **86.3 mm — 2.9σ — outside a measured height**. **AND MY OWN GUARD DID NOT CATCH IT BECAUSE I SET ITS FLOOR TO ADMIT IT:** `PHOTO_CHORD_MIN = 0.800`, commented *"under BOTH frames' lower bounds"* — **29 mm below the weaker of the two bounds its own failure text quotes**. A bar chosen to pass the value the same edit was shipping. **Rule 6, in a guard written to satisfy rule 13** | **WHAT REPLACES IT, AND WHAT IS STILL OPEN.** The chord floor is **deleted** and a **TIP-HEIGHT arm** takes its place, because tip height is **the only quantity on this board with an independent measured value**: `|tip_z − 2.184| > 3σ` fails. **WATCHED FAILING at the rev-70 candidate: tip 2.2790, +95.0 mm, 3.2σ.** The angle band widened **±4.0 → ±6.0** because on the primary frame the board's own estimators spread ~5° (two end picks **38.8°**, the navy upper stripe **43.7°**, rms 0.74 px over 32 columns) and a tighter band encodes ONE estimator. **AND THE REAL CONFLICT IS NOW ON THE RECORD RATHER THAN AVERAGED AWAY:** two-height closure **0.710**, calibrated chord read **0.790**, F165 **0.829** — the first two are BOTH from `ref_side.jpg` and disagree by **11 %**. **One of them is wrong and nothing in this tree resolves it. Do not average them** — that is exactly what I did | **MEASURED-rev70, RETRACTED-rev70** |

## Added at rev 71 — THE EMBLEM'S CONTROL DOES NOT CONTROL ITS MEASUREMENT

| ID | finding | state | grade |
|---|---|---|---|
| **F246** | **`probe_rev69_fitpose.py`'s CONTROL WAS NOT FRAMED THE WAY ITS MEASUREMENTS ARE — AND THE FIRST DRAFT OF THIS ROW GOT THE CONSEQUENCE WRONG.** `photo_mark` ends every real target with `m[ys.min():ys.max()+1, xs.min():xs.max()+1]` — **every photograph is bbox-cropped.** P1's `synth` is not: it is the raw output of `warp`, centred on the OUTPUT FRAME. And `fit()` searched **no translation**, while its docstring asserted the premise that makes that safe — *"both masks are already centred on their own bounding boxes"* — **false for a bbox crop**, because a projected disc's bbox centre is not its centre. **Bbox-framed, the control collapsed 0.9882 → 0.4988, BELOW the 0.7345 the specimen scored. A control losing to its own specimen is not a ceiling (rule 42).** | **⚠ AND THEN I CONCLUDED, WRONGLY, THAT IT COULD NOT BE REPAIRED. A DISPATCHED RULE-17 ADVERSARY REFUTED ME.** The first draft measured translation lifting the control only **0.4988 → 0.5403**, called it *"NECESSARY and NOT SUFFICIENT"*, shipped the probe REFUSING, and told rev 72 **"DO NOT MOVE ANY EMBLEM CONSTANT UNTIL P1b PASSES"** — **a blocker on the owner's #1 item that did not exist.** The adversary measured the **START SET**: `fit`'s rotations ran 0…150°, half the circle. **TWO defects were compounding, not one.** Re-measured on the same bbox-framed control: **6-param/6 starts 0.4988; 8-param/6 starts 0.5403; 8-param/FULL CIRCLE every 20° 0.9703; analytic 1.000000** | **REPAIRED AND SHIPPED, NOT DEFERRED. `fit()` now searches TRANSLATION over the FULL CIRCLE. P1b PASSES at 0.9703.** `T1_FITPOSE_LEGACY=1` restores the rev-69 search exactly and **drives P1b back to 0.4988 — WATCHED, so the repair is proven and not asserted** (rule 3), and two `verify_clone.sh` rows hold both halves. **WHAT THE REPAIRED RULER SAYS:** the mark scores **0.8425** on `ref_workshop.jpg` and **0.8215** on `IMG_2073.jpeg` at the re-cut box, against P1b's honest ceiling, which is **0.9146 at the shipped geometry** — **a real shape deficit of ≈0.07, not the 0.2537 the record published and not "unknown".** **F237's *"4.4 % of the deficit"* was a ratio against a number that was not what it claimed; its DIRECTION survives, its MAGNITUDE does not** | **MEASURED-rev71, CORRECTED-rev71** |
| **F247** | **THE TAIL-BOARD GUARD RECORDED A "WATCHED FAILING" FIGURE THAT ITS OWN BAR WOULD HAVE PASSED — RULE 5, INSIDE REV 70's OWN FIX FOR REV 70's REGRESSION.** `verify._tail_board_pose` carried *"WATCHED FAILING at the rev-70 candidate 0.8250, which put the tip at 2.2703 — 86.3 mm, 2.9 sigma outside the band"*, and `t1_shell.py` repeated it. **The bar is `TIP_BAND * 3.0` = 90.0 mm. 86.3 < 90.0 — that figure PASSES.** So the guard's docstring recorded as a watched failure a value the guard admits | **AND THE CAUSE IS A RULER MISMATCH INSIDE THE DOCSTRING THAT WARNS ABOUT RULER MISMATCHES (rule 38).** `2.2703` is `tail_board()`'s **BUILD LOG** tip — the spine endpoint. The row reads `max(p.z for p in P)`, the **MESH** maximum, which additionally carries `TB_T` and the rounded corner: **2.2790**. The two differ by **8.7 mm** and rev 70 transcribed one into a comment about the other. **The same 8.7 mm sits under the SHIPPED build's margin: it reads tip 2.2087 = +24.7 mm = 0.82σ, of which the bookkeeping offset is a third** | **RE-WATCHED AT REV 71 AND THE REAL FIGURE IS RECORDED:** `T1_TB_CHORD=0.8250` → `tail board pose … tip z 2.2790` → **`VERIFY: 1 fail`, "+95.0 mm, 3.2 sigma"**. Corrected in **both** files with the cause named. **The retraction still stands** — the candidate fails — but it fails by **4.9 mm on a 90 mm bar**, not by the 2.9σ margin the record claimed, and that is a much thinner refutation than rev 70 published | **MEASURED-rev71** |
| **F248** | **`judge_set.sh` — THE BRIEF'S ONLY PHOTOREALISM STEP — EXITED 2 AND NEVER POST-PROCESSED THE DELIVERY VIEW.** It looped `for v in front side hero hero34r`, and **no preview list has produced a plain `hero` since the `hero34f`/`hero34r` split.** So `out/<pfx>_hero.png` never exists, `set -e` plus its own `exit 2` fired after `front` and `side`, **`hero34r` was never reached and `hero34f` — the delivery frame, the view `gloss_compare.py` and `visibility_budget.py` both read — was not in the loop at all.** `HANDOFF_CARRIERS.md` §6 documented the trap at rev 69; **the documentation was fixed and the script never was**, and `audit_brief.py` cannot see it because it classes `out/rNN_hero.png` as an untracked file that legitimately starts empty | **FIXED: `for v in front side hero34f hero34r`.** Runs to **exit 0** and writes all four `_post` frames, including `out/r71_hero34f_post.png`, which this chain had never produced. **CONSEQUENCE FOR THE RECORD: every revision since the split that reported judging photorealism on the `_post` set judged it on `front` and `side` only, or not at all** | **MEASURED-rev71** |
| **F249** | **F134's NULL IS REFUTED AND BOTH ITS LEVERS ARE MEASURED INERT — THE FESTOON BULBS CANNOT BE LIT WITH WHAT THIS TREE HOLDS.** `t1_mats.py` has carried the symptom since **rev 8** (*"the drip-rail bulb string renders unlit pearl white … in both in-service photographs the bulbs are LIT and read warm"*), and rev 61 answered it with two ablation switches and a null hypothesis — *"the emission contributes nothing at studio exposure and we are seeing the base lit by the cyclorama"*. **TEN REVISIONS AND NEITHER SWITCH WAS EVER RUN.** Rev 71 ran them. **The null is REFUTED: `T1_BULB_STR` 9 → 0 moves the bead from saturation 0.0417 / V 0.9487 to 0.0251 / 0.8516. The emission arrives.** What it cannot do is COLOUR the bead: at strength 9 the bead sits at **V 0.95**, the top of the tone curve, where the view transform's path-to-white takes the chroma out | **AND THE ENVELOPE IS NOT THE SWAMP EITHER, WHICH THE SOURCE COMMENT GUESSED IT WAS.** `T1_BULB_BASEV` **1.0 → 0.05 — a 20× darker envelope — moves saturation only 0.0417 → 0.0494.** **THE PAIRING NEVER TRIED (rule 53's shape): strength AND envelope together.** Swept: (9, 0.05) 0.0494; (6, 0.05) 0.0524; **(3, 0.05) 0.0572 / V 0.8770 — the best, and it moves BOTH statistics toward the photograph**; (1.5, 0.05) 0.0329. **Against a photographed 0.1839 the best configuration is still 3.2× too neutral, and it buys +0.0155 of a ≥0.142 deficit — about 11 %** | **NOT SHIPPED, AND THE REASON IS RULE 1: I RENDERED IT, CROPPED IT AND LOOKED, AND IT DOES NOT READ.** Held beside the shipped board at 4×, (3.0, 0.05) is barely distinguishable — a cosmetic tweak, not lit bulbs. **Shipping it would have been a measured number standing in for a visible fix.** *"It cannot be recovered from the levers we hold"* is the result (rule 12). **WHAT IS LEFT: the emission COLOUR (not switchable) and the view transform's highlight desaturation — which is F240's surviving branch.** **THE GATE EXISTS NOW: `probe_rev71_bulbs.py`, B2 watched failing at 4.4×, with an ablation KILL (B3) that proves the window reads bulbs and not board** | **MEASURED-rev71** |
| **F250** | **TWO OF MY OWN BULB WINDOWS WERE WRONG AND PAINTING CAUGHT BOTH — RULE 8, TWICE IN ONE MEASUREMENT.** **(1)** The first cut selected bright-and-warm pixels in the `ref_side.jpg` crop. **Painted, it selected the pale WALL AND THE RED MURAL BEHIND THE BUS** — rev 70's exact defect (F242), repeated one revision later by someone who had just read the row recording it. It published saturation 0.218 before the paint was looked at; **discarded.** **(2)** The second cut was an 8 px corridor along the board's edge. Painted, it sits on the board — but it is **mostly BOARD CREAM**, and the emission ablation moved its mean by **0.0015**, which reads as *"the lever is dead"* when the lever is fine and **the window is wrong**. It published *"render 0.035 against photo 0.359, a 10× deficit"*; **discarded** | **THE THIRD CUT, AND WHY IT IS HONEST.** Inside the corridor, the **brightest 25 %** — the bead cores. **The pick is by BRIGHTNESS and the report is SATURATION, so it is not circular (rule 6)**, and the **same rule runs on both sides (rule 38)**. Located independently by the ablation difference itself (the pixels `T1_BULB_STR` actually moves), painted, and looked at. **AND THE PHOTOGRAPH WINDOW HAD THE CIRCULARITY DEFECT TOO**: cut (1) selected *warm* pixels and then reported how warm they were — a tautology in the target, not just the specimen | **CEILING CARRIED INTO THE GATE: both windows still contain some of the board's own cream face, which dilutes BOTH sides toward neutral, so the photographed 0.1839 is a LOWER BOUND and the deficit is a LOWER BOUND. The photograph's brightest beads are additionally R-CLIPPED (they read (255,251,99)).** `probe_rev71_bulbs.py` says all of this in its own header, so the next context cannot quote the number without the ceiling. **⚠ AND A FOURTH DEFECT, FOUND BY A DISPATCHED ADVERSARY AFTER THE PROBE SHIPPED, DESTROYS THE MAGNITUDE: the window is on the WRONG SIDE of the bead row on BOTH frames.** The emission's centre of mass sits at signed distance **d = −0.56 px** from the corridor's line; the window starts at **|d| = 1.0 and runs away from it**, so 22 % of its pixels carry almost no emission. And the two `side` signs were picked independently — on the photograph the **mirrored** side of the same line reads **0.4662 against this window's 0.1839.** The ratio moves with placement: **4.4× shipped, 9.3× emission-centred, 87.8× symmetric.** **THE DIRECTION SURVIVES EVERY PLACEMENT; THE MAGNITUDE DOES NOT — do not quote 4.4×, "3.2× too neutral" or "11 % of the deficit" as measurements of the vehicle.** Recorded in the probe's own header. **This is the FOURTH window rule 8 killed in one revision, and the first one a script found rather than a painting** | **MEASURED-rev71, CEILED-rev71** |
| **F251** | **THE EMBLEM'S FIT DEPTH IS MEASURED — the item three briefs called *"still UNMEASURED"* and dropped three times — AND IT WAS NOT ALREADY RIGHT.** `_fit_glyph(obs, ring_r * (1.0 − 0.8·_BAND_FRAC))` = **0.84 R** against a band inner edge of 0.80 R, i.e. the glyph's extreme driven 20 % into the band. **⚠ AND IT IS IN `t1_detail.vw_logo_fit`, NOT `t1_core.py` where the rev-71 brief cited it** — `grep -n "BAND_FRAC" t1_core.py` returns nothing (rule 18) | **⚠ THE FIRST DRAFT OF THIS ROW GRADED IT `CLOSED — it was already right`, AND THAT WAS MEASURED ON THE BROKEN RULER (F246).** On the repaired search **both photographs prefer a DEEPER fit**: **both frames' argmax is 0.86**, so the shipped 0.84 was one sweep step BELOW both. `probe_rev71_emblem.py`'s **E1 REFUSES** when the shipped value is more than one step from either argmax — **and it correctly refused the 0.84 it had just passed on the broken ruler.** *(A dispatched adversary predicted this before the sweep was re-run.)* | **CLOSED BY SHIPPING IT — SEE F256.** `VW_FIT_COEF` 0.8 → **0.7**, i.e. FIT_R 0.84 → **0.86**, `T1_VW_FITCOEF=0.8` restoring the old value exactly. **AND THE ENABLING INSTRUMENT IS KEPT: `probe_rev71_proxy.py`, a 2-D replica of the emblem raster PROVEN BIT-IDENTICAL to the bpy build** — IoU **1.000000**, identical on-pixel counts, **and re-verified at SEVEN perturbed spine parameters one at a time by a dispatched adversary, plus correctly BREAKING (0.913/0.895) when the stroke weight differs, so it is not trivially matching.** ~0.01 s an evaluation against ~2 s. **ONE CEILING: the `on_band=False` branch that F252's (B) and (C) use has NO bpy counterpart — "proven" covers the constrained construction only** | **MEASURED-rev71, CORRECTED-rev71** |
| **F252** | **THE CONSTRUCTION CHANGE THE REV-71 BRIEF PRESCRIBES DOES NOT CLOSE THE EMBLEM'S RESIDUAL — MEASURED THREE WAYS.** The brief's §3 BUILD instruction is *"give each stroke its **own centreline with free endpoints**, not forced onto the band circle, so the six strokes can be made near-parallel"*, on rule 54's mechanism. **Run in the proven proxy, all scored by `probe_rev69_fitpose.fit()`, fitting on `ref_workshop.jpg` and scoring on `IMG_2073.jpeg` at the re-cut box:** **(A)** the CURRENT parameterisation re-searched — **0.7345 → 0.7488** (+0.0143, the same order as F237's published +0.0112) but the independent frame **FALLS 0.6553 → 0.5700**: that optimum is overfit. **(B)** the brief's prescription, terminals freed from the band circle, seeded at the shipped mark — **0.7478 / 0.5702**, i.e. **0.0010 WORSE than (A) on the fit frame** and level on the independent one. **(C)** the same free construction under a 1400-start global search with polish — **0.7586 fit / 0.6698 independent** — **re-run from the committed path it reproduces EXACTLY (502 s)**, the only one of the three that improves BOTH frames | **SO THE PARAMETERISATION IS NOT THE BINDING CONSTRAINT: FREEING IT BUYS NOTHING — (B) is 0.0010 BELOW (A) on the frame it was fitted to.** Every basin the global search found plateaus near **0.75**. **Rule 54's PRESCRIPTION is refuted by measurement; its OBSERVATION — that the built strokes radiate where the photograph's are near-parallel — stands, and is visible in the painted overlay** (`probe_scratch/rev69_fitpose.png`: the residual is stroke-by-stroke lateral displacement, not a global scale or rotation) | **AND EVERY FIGURE IN THIS ROW WAS COMPUTED ON THE BROKEN RULER (F246) AND HAS NOT BEEN RE-RUN ON THE REPAIRED ONE.** All six figures are computed on the instrument F246 shows is mis-controlled, so they RANK candidates against each other on one fixed ruler — which is what a sweep needs — but **their distance from 0.9882 is not a shape deficit.** (C) is a real +0.024/+0.015 by that ruler and **was NOT shipped**: moving vehicle geometry on a ruler whose control refuses is rev 70's mistake exactly (F245). **AND THE SEARCHES ARE REPRODUCIBLE FROM THE TREE, NOT ONLY FROM THIS ROW: `T1_REV71_SEARCH=AB python3 probe_rev71_emblem.py` (~40 s) prints (A) and (B);
